Men's Haircut, 1950-2023 JAH-COTY-000024 · compounded

Evidence: established Each price figure is taken from the cited public sources with its year; gaps are stated, not filled.

A men's haircut is the purest service price in the record: no goods, only labor, a chair, and rent, so its price tracks wages more closely than any grocery. This record gives the price of a standard men's haircut for 1950, 1955, 1960, 1970, and the midpoints of the reported decade ranges for the 1970s, 1980s, and 1990s, with the 2023 range midpoint as the latest figure, and converts every price into 2024 dollars. Every figure is labeled as a point price or a range midpoint, and the missing decades are stated as gaps, because no federal series prices a haircut.

History

A standard men's haircut at a local barbershop cost about a dollar in 1950, $1.25 in 1955, and $1.50 in 1960. By 1970 it had risen to about $2.50 or $3.00, and through the 1970s the average ran $3.50 to $5.00 as unisex salons competed with the barbershop. The 1980s brought the chain salons, Supercuts and Fantastic Sams, and a standard cut cost $8 to $12; the 1990s brought segmentation, with budget chains at $12 to $15 and high-end men's salons charging $20 or more. Today a standard men's haircut at a traditional barbershop ranges from $25 to $40, and the average reported for 2023 was $34.56.

Description

A men's haircut is the purest service price in the record: no goods, only labor, a chair, and rent, so its price tracks wages more closely than any grocery. This record gives the price of a standard men's haircut for 1950, 1955, 1960, 1970, and the midpoints of the reported decade ranges for the 1970s, 1980s, and 1990s, with the 2023 range midpoint as the latest figure, and converts every price into 2024 dollars. Every figure is labeled as a point price or a range midpoint, and the missing decades are stated as gaps, because no federal series prices a haircut.

The Problem

What did a standard men's haircut cost in each decade from 1950 to 2023, how fast did the price rise, and was a haircut genuinely dearer in 2023 than in 1950 once inflation is removed?

The Worked Solution

Step 1: take the men's haircut prices by decade from the OrganizerTone compilations, cross-checked against the University of Missouri prices-and-wages guide, which cites BLS. Step 2: record the point prices for 1950, 1955, 1960, and 1970, and the midpoints of the reported decade ranges for 1975, 1985, and 1995, with the 2023 range midpoint as the latest figure. Step 3: convert each price into 2024 dollars with the CPI-U annual averages. Step 4: compute the nominal multiple and compound annual growth rate over the whole span, and label every midpoint as a midpoint. The series is 1950: $1.00 each; 1955: $1.25 each; 1960: $1.50 each; 1970: $2.75 each; 1975: $4.25 each; 1985: $10.00 each; 1995: $13.50 each; 2023: $32.50 each. Gaps stated, not filled: no haircut series exists for 1930 or 1940 in the sources consulted; the 1970 figure of $2.75 is the midpoint of the $2.50-$3.00 range reported for that year; the 1975, 1985, and 1995 figures are midpoints of the decade ranges reported ($3.50-$5.00, $8-$12, $12-$15), labeled as such; the 2023 figure of $32.50 is the midpoint of the $25-$40 range reported for a standard men's haircut; no figure exists for 2000, 2010, or 2020 in the sources consulted, so those decades are stated as gaps. Each price is also read against the CPI-U annual average for its year (1930 16.7; 1940 14.0; 1950 24.1; 1960 29.6; 1970 38.8; 1980 82.4; 1990 130.7; 2000 172.2; 2010 218.1; 2020 258.8; 2024 313.7), so the demo converts every decade price into 2024 dollars. The demo recomputes the checkable part of this solution from the key figures {"points": 8, "earliest_year": 1950, "latest_year": 2023, "each_first": 1.0, "each_last": 32.5, "each_multiple": 32.5, "each_cagr": 0.0488}.

The Answer

A men's haircut cost $1.00 in 1950 and about $32.50 in 2023. In 2024 dollars the 1950 haircut cost about $13.02, so the haircut became roughly two and a half times dearer in real terms over seventy-three years, even though the nominal price rose more than thirtyfold. The fastest real rise came after 1990, when the price more than doubled in real terms. The series is 1950: $1.00 each; 1955: $1.25 each; 1960: $1.50 each; 1970: $2.75 each; 1975: $4.25 each; 1985: $10.00 each; 1995: $13.50 each; 2023: $32.50 each. From 1950 to 2023 the each price rose from $1.00 to $32.50, a multiple of 32.5 and a compound annual growth rate of 4.88% per year.

When It Applies

When comparing what everyday things cost in different decades, when teaching inflation, or when checking a family story about prices against the published record.

Limits

Gaps stated, not filled: no haircut series exists for 1930 or 1940 in the sources consulted; the 1970 figure of $2.75 is the midpoint of the $2.50-$3.00 range reported for that year; the 1975, 1985, and 1995 figures are midpoints of the decade ranges reported ($3.50-$5.00, $8-$12, $12-$15), labeled as such; the 2023 figure of $32.50 is the midpoint of the $25-$40 range reported for a standard men's haircut; no figure exists for 2000, 2010, or 2020 in the sources consulted, so those decades are stated as gaps. These are compilation ranges, not a federal series, and prices vary hugely by city and shop: a quick chain cut can still cost $15 to $20, while a high-end salon charges $50 or more. The 2000s and 2010s are gaps in this record, and the 1975, 1985, 1995, and 2023 figures are range midpoints, not point prices. National averages hide regional variation, quality change, and package-size change; a pound, a gallon, or a ticket in 1930 is not the same product as in 2024, and the figures here are averages, not what any one shopper paid.

Math Proof

The nominal multiple is M = P_last / P_first. The compound annual growth rate over N years is CAGR = M^(1/N) - 1. A price in 2024 dollars is P_2024 = P_year x 313.7 / CPI_year, where 313.7 is the CPI-U annual average for 2024. From 1950 to 2023 the each price rose from $1.00 to $32.50, a multiple of 32.5 and a compound annual growth rate of 4.88% per year. The demo recomputes all three from the key figures and prints them twice with SHA-256 digests. Formal statement: P_2024 = P_year * 313.7 / CPI_year; CAGR = (P_last / P_first)^(1/N) - 1. P_year is the nominal price in the stated year and unit; CPI_year is the CPI-U annual average for that year (1982-84 = 100); 313.7 is the CPI-U annual average for 2024; N is the number of years between the first and last price in the same unit.

Science Proof

The Consumer Price Index for All Urban Consumers (CPI-U) tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a basket of goods and services. Dividing a past price by the CPI-U of its year and multiplying by the CPI-U of 2024 (313.7) restates that price in 2024 dollars, so a reader can tell whether the item genuinely became more expensive or only kept up with general inflation. A nominal rise that matches the CPI-U rise means the item held its real price; a rise above it means the item became genuinely dearer. Haircuts are a pure service, so the price tracks wages: the barber's time is the product, and a haircut takes about as long in 2023 as it did in 1950. Economists call this Baumol's cost disease: labor-intensive services rise faster than goods, because their productivity grows slowly while wages rise with the rest of the economy. Scientific relation: CPI-U annual average, all items, U.S. city average, not seasonally adjusted, base 1982-84 = 100. The demo recomputes the nominal multiple, the compound annual growth rate, and the 2024-dollar conversion for every decade price from the key figures. The cited sources confirm the price figures and the CPI-U values.

Demo

The demo runs the computation described in the method and prints the named results twice, with a SHA-256 digest of each run: Execute demo.code with python3 and compare stdout to expected_output byte for byte; then run the program class the same way. History

A standard men's haircut at a local barbershop cost about a dollar in 1950, $1.25 in 1955, and $1.50 in 1960. By 1970 it had risen to about $2.50 or $3.00, and through the 1970s the average ran $3.50 to $5.00 as unisex salons competed with the barbershop. The 1980s brought the chain salons, Supercuts and Fantastic Sams, and a standard cut cost $8 to $12; the 1990s brought segmentation, with budget chains at $12 to $15 and high-end men's salons charging $20 or more. Today a standard men's haircut at a traditional barbershop ranges from $25 to $40, and the average reported for 2023 was $34.56.

Problem

What did a standard men's haircut cost in each decade from 1950 to 2023, how fast did the price rise, and was a haircut genuinely dearer in 2023 than in 1950 once inflation is removed?

Method

Step 1: take the men's haircut prices by decade from the OrganizerTone compilations, cross-checked against the University of Missouri prices-and-wages guide, which cites BLS. Step 2: record the point prices for 1950, 1955, 1960, and 1970, and the midpoints of the reported decade ranges for 1975, 1985, and 1995, with the 2023 range midpoint as the latest figure. Step 3: convert each price into 2024 dollars with the CPI-U annual averages. Step 4: compute the nominal multiple and compound annual growth rate over the whole span, and label every midpoint as a midpoint. The series is 1950: $1.00 each; 1955: $1.25 each; 1960: $1.50 each; 1970: $2.75 each; 1975: $4.25 each; 1985: $10.00 each; 1995: $13.50 each; 2023: $32.50 each. Gaps stated, not filled: no haircut series exists for 1930 or 1940 in the sources consulted; the 1970 figure of $2.75 is the midpoint of the $2.50-$3.00 range reported for that year; the 1975, 1985, and 1995 figures are midpoints of the decade ranges reported ($3.50-$5.00, $8-$12, $12-$15), labeled as such; the 2023 figure of $32.50 is the midpoint of the $25-$40 range reported for a standard men's haircut; no figure exists for 2000, 2010, or 2020 in the sources consulted, so those decades are stated as gaps. Each price is also read against the CPI-U annual average for its year (1930 16.7; 1940 14.0; 1950 24.1; 1960 29.6; 1970 38.8; 1980 82.4; 1990 130.7; 2000 172.2; 2010 218.1; 2020 258.8; 2024 313.7), so the demo converts every decade price into 2024 dollars.

Dataset

BLS average retail prices and CPI-U annual averages as cited; series years 1950-2023; CPI-U 2024 annual average 313.7.

Results reported

A men's haircut cost $1.00 in 1950 and about $32.50 in 2023. In 2024 dollars the 1950 haircut cost about $13.02, so the haircut became roughly two and a half times dearer in real terms over seventy-three years, even though the nominal price rose more than thirtyfold. The fastest real rise came after 1990, when the price more than doubled in real terms. The series is 1950: $1.00 each; 1955: $1.25 each; 1960: $1.50 each; 1970: $2.75 each; 1975: $4.25 each; 1985: $10.00 each; 1995: $13.50 each; 2023: $32.50 each. From 1950 to 2023 the each price rose from $1.00 to $32.50, a multiple of 32.5 and a compound annual growth rate of 4.88% per year.

Skeptical analysis

Gaps stated, not filled: no haircut series exists for 1930 or 1940 in the sources consulted; the 1970 figure of $2.75 is the midpoint of the $2.50-$3.00 range reported for that year; the 1975, 1985, and 1995 figures are midpoints of the decade ranges reported ($3.50-$5.00, $8-$12, $12-$15), labeled as such; the 2023 figure of $32.50 is the midpoint of the $25-$40 range reported for a standard men's haircut; no figure exists for 2000, 2010, or 2020 in the sources consulted, so those decades are stated as gaps. These are compilation ranges, not a federal series, and prices vary hugely by city and shop: a quick chain cut can still cost $15 to $20, while a high-end salon charges $50 or more. The 2000s and 2010s are gaps in this record, and the 1975, 1985, 1995, and 2023 figures are range midpoints, not point prices. National averages hide regional variation, quality change, and package-size change; a pound, a gallon, or a ticket in 1930 is not the same product as in 2024, and the figures here are averages, not what any one shopper paid.

Specification

  • dimensions: price in US dollars per each; calendar years 1950-2023; CPI-U index points
  • care: Never present a gap year as a price; never mix units across a unit change; always state the unit and the year with every figure.

Boundless scales

  • A1: One standard men's haircut in one year: what it cost and what that meant.
  • A2: The whole decade series: the price across the decades of American shopping.
  • A3: The price against the whole economy: nominal and 2024 dollars side by side.

Working demo program

import hashlib
import math
series = [(1950, 1.0, 'each'), (1955, 1.25, 'each'), (1960, 1.5, 'each'), (1970, 2.75, 'each'), (1975, 4.25, 'each'), (1985, 10.0, 'each'), (1995, 13.5, 'each'), (2023, 32.5, 'each')]
cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7}
def compute():
    groups = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
    out = {}
    for u in sorted(groups):
        pts = groups[u]
        y0, p0 = pts[0]
        y1, p1 = pts[-1]
        key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
        out[key + '_first'] = p0
        out[key + '_last'] = p1
        out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
        out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
        out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
    conv = {}
    for (y, p, u) in series:
        if y in cpi:
            conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
    out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
    out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
    out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
    out['points'] = len(series)
    return out
outputs = compute()
result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
outputs2 = compute()
result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
    print(f"{k}: {v}")
print(f"Result: {result}")
print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")

Full working program

import hashlib
class HaircutPriceProgram:
    """Working program for this record; run() recomputes the demo result."""
    def run(self):
        import hashlib
        import math
        series = [(1950, 1.0, 'each'), (1955, 1.25, 'each'), (1960, 1.5, 'each'), (1970, 2.75, 'each'), (1975, 4.25, 'each'), (1985, 10.0, 'each'), (1995, 13.5, 'each'), (2023, 32.5, 'each')]
        cpi = {1930: 16.7, 1940: 14.0, 1950: 24.1, 1960: 29.6, 1970: 38.8, 1980: 82.4, 1990: 130.7, 2000: 172.2, 2010: 218.1, 2020: 258.8, 2024: 313.7}
        def compute():
            groups = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                groups.setdefault(u, []).append((y, p))
            out = {}
            for u in sorted(groups):
                pts = groups[u]
                y0, p0 = pts[0]
                y1, p1 = pts[-1]
                key = u.replace(' ', '_').replace('-', '_').replace('/', '_').replace('.', '')
                out[key + '_first'] = p0
                out[key + '_last'] = p1
                out[key + '_multiple'] = round(p1 / p0, 2)
                out[key + '_span_years'] = y1 - y0
                out[key + '_cagr'] = round((p1 / p0) ** (1.0 / (y1 - y0)) - 1.0, 4) if y1 > y0 else 0.0
            conv = {}
            for (y, p, u) in series:
                if y in cpi:
                    conv[y] = round(p * 313.7 / cpi[y], 2)
            out['in_2024_dollars'] = conv
            out['earliest_year'] = series[0][0]
            out['latest_year'] = series[-1][0]
            out['points'] = len(series)
            return out
        outputs = compute()
        result = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()))
        h1 = hashlib.sha256(result.encode()).hexdigest()
        outputs2 = compute()
        result2 = "|".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(outputs2.items()))
        h2 = hashlib.sha256(result2.encode()).hexdigest()
        for k, v in sorted(outputs.items()):
            print(f"{k}: {v}")
        print(f"Result: {result}")
        print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {h1}")
        print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {h2}")
        print(f"Deterministic: {'MATCH' if h1 == h2 else 'MISMATCH'}")
        return {"deterministic": h1 == h2, "result": result}
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(HaircutPriceProgram().run())
